## J. B. Kimsey

### Summary
The author of the 1951 paper on kokanee spawning in Donner Lake, affiliated with the Bureau of Fish Conservation, California Division of Fish and Game. The source reports that he documented the 1949 field observations, found that eggs in seepage areas and some frozen nests survived, and concluded that large-scale natural reproduction would be possible if lake water levels were stabilized. (The source citation lists the author's initial as R., while the text gives J. B.)

### Context
> Field observations from the fall of 1949 examined whether [[Kokanee]] (*Oncorhynchus nerka kennerlyi*) spawning on the shore of [[Donner Lake]], Nevada County, California, could produce viable offspring. Spawning began in early November 1949. Nests were exposed by a severe drawdown in December, and...
> -- *[[NotesOnKokaneeSpawningInDonner_12527]]*

### Summary

## J. G. Edwards

### Summary
J. G. Edwards wrote earlier pieces on subspecies, including a 1956 article in Turtox News that defines subspecies as "recognizably different demes" kept apart during mating. ## Jacob Corwin Blumer

### Summary
Jacob Corwin Blumer (1872-1947) was a Swiss-born botanist who conducted extensive fieldwork in southeastern Arizona Territory between August 1906 and October 1907. During this 10-month period, he traveled more than 1,600 miles and collected approximately 1,050 plant species (including 25 new to science), making the first extensive plant collections in the Chiricahua Mountains. He was a pioneering plant ecologist who articulated important principles about species distribution being limited by moisture at low elevations and temperature at high elevations, and about how species plasticity enables wider distribution ranges.

## James River
<!-- fm: auto_enriched: true -->

### Summary
The James River is a major Atlantic Slope drainage system in Virginia that serves as an important ichthyological study area for understanding fish diversity and migratory species dynamics along the eastern United States. As an anadromous fish habitat and estuarine system, the James River has been systematically surveyed for fish populations and serves as a key reference site for documenting species distributions across multiple drainages in the Central Atlantic region. The river's ichthyofauna includes significant populations of sturgeon species and represents an important ecological zone for conservation efforts related to Atlantic Slope fish communities.

## Jamestown Falls

### Summary
A natural waterfall and rapids on the Raquette River that, with Carry Falls, formed a barrier to upstream fish movement before impoundment. The Jamestown stillwater section above the rapids retained rooted aquatic vegetation after the reservoir was filled, since it experienced less water-level fluctuation than the main body.

Jarvis I (1969) held that reasonable use barred moving water off the land of origin where neighbors were harmed. Jarvis II (1970) allowed Tucson to export water equal to the amount not used on retired farmland in a Critical Groundwater Area, and Jarvis III (1976) limited exports to the consumptive use of that farmland, about half of the historical pumpage.

### Context
> This Project Bulletin (January 1977) by attorney Robert Fleming summarizes the historical development of [[Groundwater Law]] in [[Arizona]]. It traces the shift from early surface-water-oriented statutes to the [[Groundwater Code of 1948]], the contested [[Bristor v. Cheatham]] decisions on private...
> -- *[[ArizonaGroundwaterLawHistorica_16464]]*

## John Otterbein Snyder
<!-- fm: auto_enriched: true -->

### Summary
John Otterbein Snyder was a prominent ichthyologist and fisheries scientist who conducted pioneering biogeographical surveys of western North American fish faunas, most notably a 1915 expedition examining the ancient Lake Bonneville basin (now the Great Salt Lake region). His work on isolated fish populations in now-desiccated basins established foundational methods for reconstructing historical fish distributions and understanding speciation patterns in western aquatic systems. Snyder's contributions were instrumental in early 20th-century ichthyological research on conservation and evolutionary biology, influencing subsequent generations of fish scientists working on relict and endemic fish populations.

## John S. Gottschalk

### Summary
Gottschalk was Director of the Bureau of Sport Fisheries and Wildlife, and his October 3, 1969 letter reports on his meeting with Mexican fisheries officials. He found that Mexico acknowledged the bass problem and planned surveys and a laboratory, but he expected commercial and sport-fishing interests to keep driving stocking. He also noted Mexican reluctance to accept domination by American scientists.

## Johnny Darter
<!-- fm: auto_enriched: true -->

### Summary
The Johnny Darter (*Etheostoma nigrum*) is a small benthic darter species commonly found in freshwater streams and rivers across North America, particularly in cool to moderate-temperature headwater and mid-reach habitats. As a member of the darter family (Percidae), it serves as an important indicator species for stream health and ecological assessments due to its sensitivity to water quality and habitat conditions. Its presence in ichthyological surveys and fish community studies reflects the structural complexity and stability of stream substrates, making it a valuable component of aquatic ecosystem monitoring and conservation biology.

## Jordan And Evermann
<!-- fm: auto_enriched: true -->

### Summary
Jordan and Evermann refers to the landmark ichthyological works produced by David Starr Jordan and Barton Warren Evermann in the late 19th and early 20th centuries (particularly 1896 and 1896–1900), which provided the first comprehensive scientific treatment and identification guide for North American freshwater fishes. Their foundational publications established critical taxonomic designations and type-species assignments for numerous fish genera, serving as the authoritative reference for fish identification and nomenclature in the United States until subsequent regional identification manuals were developed in the mid-20th century. The validity and influence of their taxonomic work remained subjects of formal scientific discussion and nomenclatural review well into the modern era of ichthyology.

### Context

## Jordan's Rule

### Summary
Jordan's Rule is the generalization, first noted by Jordan in 1891, that many fish groups have fewer vertebrae nearer the tropics and more in cold, deep, dark and fresh waters. This source says the rule concerns cold-water or northern representatives of certain groups, and it should not be confused with Jordan's Law about closely related forms in adjacent areas. The emerald shiner counts reported here run opposite to the pattern the rule predicts, with more vertebrae toward the river mouth at lower latitude.

### Context

### Summary
The June Sucker Recovery Implementation Program is a collaborative management entity involving the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, Utah Division of Wildlife Resources, U.S. Bureau of Reclamation, Central Utah Water Conservancy District, and other agencies working to recover the federally endangered June sucker. ## Justus F. Mueller

### Summary
Justus F. Mueller was a field naturalist who wrote this paper describing the urinary ciliate Trichodina renicola from chain pickerel in Oneida Lake, New York. He reassigned the organism from Cyclochaeta to Trichodina on the basis of its lack of cirri and documented its morphology, host specificity and pathology.

### Context
> This paper by [[Justus F. Mueller]], a field naturalist at the [[Roosevelt Wild Life Station]], formally describes a peritrichous ciliate that lives inside the urinary system of the [[Chain Pickerel]] (*Esox niger*) in [[Oneida Lake]], New York. The organism was first published as *Cyclochaeta...

## Juvenile Impingement

### Summary
Impingement is when fish are pinned against a screen by water flowing toward the diversion. The source says juvenile winter-run salmon were impinged when screen approach velocities were too high, a problem made worse when falling river levels reduced the effective screen area.

### Context

## Juvenile Salmon

### Summary
Young salmon in their early life stages, used here as test fish in estuarine toxicity bioassays. The source notes they were held in live-boxes and observed for distress or mortality as water conditions changed. In one episode, they died after sulfides from decomposing sludge appeared in the water.

### Context

## Juxtaposition of Habitat Components

### Summary
Juxtaposition is the idea that habitat requires food, cover and water to be arranged in relation to one another according to a species' life needs, not just present individually. Parker uses this analogy to explain why map topology matters in GIS.

### Context
